# Extraction and Verification Audit

Source enumeration and admission certainty are separate: an identified atom has a recorded disposition, but unresolved and excluded atoms are not admitted Questions. A note's verified flag is not by itself visual/source evidence; the per-ID register below exposes each recorded check and any missing evidence.

Prompt fingerprints and Chinese checkpoints are structural/regression safeguards, not automatic semantic proof. They do not replace direct PDF comparison, mathematical/model review, or native Obsidian rendering checks.

## Documented Source Answer Corrections

The printed claims and their corrections are retained below. Provenance is item-specific: changed conclusions or independently completed proofs are derived; secondary notation, arithmetic, label or cross-reference slips may retain a correct official conclusion and complete underlying argument. Unresolved records are not admitted answers.

| ID | Source PDF | Page | Answer provenance | Source defect and correction |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| Q-2013-MID-P06-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2013-midterm.pdf]] | 9 | not admitted | The printed 0.6322 differs from 1-(119/120)^120=0.6336587344... in the intended iid model. Neither uniform draws nor independent days is supplied, so this numeric correction is audit-only and the atom is needs-review. |
| Q-2014-FINAL-P08 | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2014-final.pdf]] | 12 | derived | PDF page 12 substitutes x=1/a in the weighted finite geometric sum but prints denominator (1-a)^2, not (1-1/a)^2. The independently derived bound uses 0≤Σ(i/a^i)≤a/(a-1)^2, yielding a^n≤T(n)≤[1+a/(a-1)^2]a^n; the literal printed denominator remains in Source. |
| Q-2015-FINAL-P08-b | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2015-final.pdf]] | 14 | derived | PDF page14 establishes n=0 but prints “Now consider n>1” in the inductive step. This omits the 0→1 transition. The corrected proof uses every n≥1 and independently verifies T(1)=33. |
| Q-2015-FINAL-P10 | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2015-final.pdf]] | 17 | derived | PDF page17 applies the hypothesis P(n−1) without restricting m≤n−1, then concludes for all m≤n. The new modulus m=n is not covered by that hypothesis. The completed induction treats it separately using n!=n(n−1)!, hence n divides n!. |
| Q-2015-MID-P02-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2015-midterm.pdf]] | 5 | official | PDF page 6 line 5 cites Modus Ponens (3,4), but line 3 is q and the necessary p is line 2. The correct references are (2,4). The formula ¬(q∧r) and final ¬s remain the supplied official conclusions; only the cross-reference is corrected. |
| Q-2016-FINAL-P03-e | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2016-final.pdf]] | 7 | derived | PDF page 7 obtains 5/6 (and joint 5/9) by multiplying the two fair-die success probabilities. Conditional independence of the two rolls is not printed. The conclusion of dependence is nevertheless forced by the stated marginal laws; the following general-coupling proof is derived, not a verification of that intended iid calculation. |
| Q-2017-MID-P01-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2017-midterm.pdf]] | 3 | official | PDF page 3 says “It is false if Q(x) is always false.” This is insufficient alone: choose a nonempty domain with every P true as well as every Q false. The official non-tautology conclusion is correct; the exam explicitly requires no counterexample for non-tautologies. |
| Q-2017-MID-P03-b | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2017-midterm.pdf]] | 5 | official | PDF page 5 prints x=(√x)²=(a/b)²=a²/b². Starting with x=a/b, the correct squared equality is x²=(a/b)²=a²/b². The official contrapositive method and conclusion are unchanged; the displayed intermediate equality is corrected. |
| Q-2017-MID-P04-a | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2017-midterm.pdf]] | 6 | official | PDF page 6 uses a=1,b=3,c=5,m=2 but finishes with 1 not congruent to 5 modulo 2. The required comparison is 1 not congruent to b+c=8 modulo 2. The printed values form a valid counterexample and the official False conclusion remains unchanged. |
| Q-2017-FINAL-P04-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2017-final.pdf]] | 6 | derived | PDF page7 bounds prime factors of n, but failed candidate primes divide T=(p−1)(q−1), not n=pq. Correct argument: the product of r distinct failed primes divides T and is at least2^r, so2^r≤T<n; therefore at most floor(log2 T)+1=O(log n) tests. This independently completes the proof without counting factors of the wrong integer or treating log2 n as an integer. |
| Q-2017-FINAL-P13-b | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2017-final.pdf]] | 17 | official | PDF page17 defines the named cycle C_n but writes consecutive-edge indices 1≤i≤n before separately giving the closing edge v_n to v_1. At i=n this mentions undefined v_(n+1). The standard cycle named in the prompt has consecutive edges for 1≤i<n and the explicit closing edge; its even-cycle conclusion remains official. The literal index slip is retained in the shared transcription, not silently repaired. |
| Q-2018-MID-P04-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2018-midterm.pdf]] | 6 | official | PDF page6 defines the compression domain as F_n and its image T⊆F_(n−1), but the final injectivity line prints \|T\|≥\|F_(n−1)\|. The cited part(a) requires \|T\|≥\|F_n\|, contradicting \|T\|<\|F_n\|. The official impossibility conclusion and pigeonhole method remain unchanged; this subscript is corrected. |
| Q-2018-MID-P07-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2018-midterm.pdf]] | 9 | official | PDF page9 lists w=5,4,3 correctly and totals6132, but then says “In all other cases, there will be more women than men in the committee.” The other cases have w≤2 and fewer women than men. Correct only that explanatory word; the official case split and formula are unchanged. |
| Q-2018-FINAL-P03-a | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2018-final.pdf]] | 5 | official | PDF page5 switches k/n in the induction step and prints −an−a=−(a+1)n and an−(−a)=(a+1)n. The correct equalities are −an−a=−a(n+1) and an−(−a)=a(n+1), or the consistent k versions. The official closure-under-integer-multiples conclusion and difference-closure induction are unchanged. |
| Q-2018-FINAL-P04-a | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2018-final.pdf]] | 6 | official | PDF page6 prints the filled basis as {1,2,3}∈X. For X a set of positive integers the intended three basis elements are1,2,3∈X, equivalently {1,2,3}⊆X; a set-valued element is not intended. The three official basis values and generation rule are retained. |
| Q-2018-FINAL-P09-a | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2018-final.pdf]] | 13 | official | PDF page13 prints 10+14+35−2−5−7+1=36 and then70−36=24. The inclusion-exclusion sum is46, and70−46=24. The official final count24 and the displayed set counts/method are correct; the intermediate36 is an arithmetic typo. |
| Q-2018-FINAL-P12-b | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2018-final.pdf]] | 17 | derived | PDF page17 gives the counterexample f(x)=2x,g(x)=x, but the prompt requires both functions from all real numbers to positive real numbers; these fail that codomain at x≤0. The corrected independent counterexample f(x)=2·2^x,g(x)=2^x is positive on all reals, satisfies f=2g, and has exponential ratio2^(2^x)→∞. The False classification is retained, but the source-invalid witness is replaced and the answer is derived. |
| Q-2019-MID-P04 | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2019-midterm.pdf]] | 6 | official | PDF page6 answer step10 prints ∃x(Q(x)∧R(w)), although the requested conclusion and the common witness in steps2–9 require R(x). Replace only R(w) by R(x) in that final existential generalization. The official validity conclusion and preceding nine inferences remain correct. |
| Q-2020-MID-P07 | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2020-midterm.pdf]] | 4 | official | PDF page4 final back-substitution line prints 1=23·(−1)+10·7, whose right side is47. Expanding the immediately preceding 1=10−(23−10·2)·3 gives 1=23·(−3)+10·7. Correct the coefficient of23 to−3; the official inverse7 and solution x≡18(mod23) are correct. |
| Q-2020-FINAL1-P04-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2020-final-part-1.pdf]] | 3 | official | PDF page3 first solution writes n=100x_1+10x_2+x_3, but immediately indexes its digit restriction by i=0,1,2. The intended digit indices are i=1,2,3, as correctly printed in the alternative solution on the same page. Correct only this index list; both official counts1+52+28 and9×9 equal81. |
| Q-2020-FINAL2-P01 | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2020-final-part-2.pdf]] | 1 | official | PDF page1 expands the second sum as Σ_(i=1)^n n!/((i−2)!(n−i)!), introducing undefined(−1)! at i=1; the next expression also uses(n−2)! and so cannot cover n=1. First discard the zero i=1 term and start at i=2 for n≥2, then check n=1 separately. The official split i²=i(i−1)+i and final identity on page2 remain correct. |
| Q-2022-MID-P07-b | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2022-midterm.pdf]] | 3 | official | Rendered PDF page3 Problem7(b) correctly asks for 3^2022 mod4, then the next intermediate statement prints 3^3022≡1(mod4). That occurrence of3022 should be2022. Since2022 is even, 3^2022≡1(mod4), and the official final value3 for 3^(3^2022) mod10 remains correct. |
| Q-2022-FINAL2-P05-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2022-final-part-2.pdf]] | 3 | official | Rendered PDF page3 P05(c) says that if there are edges between two vertices in V1, then G is not bipartite. That only shows the displayed partition fails, unless further conditions exclude all other partitions. The source permits V2 empty: V1={a,b},V2=∅ and edgeab satisfies(a)(ii) vacuously yet is K2 and bipartite under a different partition. The official overall No remains correct: with nonempty V1={a,b},V2={c}, edgesab,ac,bc form K3, satisfy(a)(ii), and rule out every bipartition. |
| Q-2023F-FINAL-P03 | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2023_Final.pdf]] | 6 | official | PDF page 6 prints 0.02% in the question, equal to 0.0002, but writes 0.00002 twice in the intermediate official calculation. Its final symbolic Bayes quotient and target event are correct. The note keeps official provenance for that structure and independently evaluates the stated rates as 49985001/49985006. |
| Q-2024S-FINAL-P04 | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_S2024_Final.pdf]] | 4 | official | PDF p5 labels the good-path expression C(11,6)−C(6,4)C(5,2) as #P2,bad; the label should be #P2. The bad-path count is150, good-path count312, and printed final10·312=3120 is correct. Page4 already defines #P2=#P2,all−#P2,bad correctly. This secondary label slip does not replace the official conclusion. |
| Q-2024F-MID-P01-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2024_Midterm.pdf]] | 3 | official | PDF p4 step8 prints ¬p from7,2. The correct line-reference is from7,6: line6 proves the antecedent of line7. All printed formulas and the final ¬p∧¬t are correct. This secondary citation slip does not replace the official conclusion. |
| Q-2025S-MID-P02-b-iv | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_S2025_Midterm.pdf]] | 4 | not admitted | Page 4 prints $\exists b(a\cdot b\equiv1\pmod m\leftrightarrow gcd(a,m)=1)$. It scopes the existential over the biconditional rather than over its left-hand existence assertion. The corrected translation explicitly uses $(\exists b(ab\equiv1\pmod m))\leftrightarrow gcd(a,m)=1$; it does not claim that every positive number-theoretic instance separates the two formulas. |
| Q-2025S-MID-P02-b-v | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_S2025_Midterm.pdf]] | 4 | derived | Page 4 prints $\forall p(prime(p)\to\forall x(x<p\to\exists y(y<p\land xy\equiv1\pmod p)))$, omitting non-zero. At p=2,x=0 it demands an impossible inverse. Add the required 0<x bound from the printed English statement. |
| Q-2025S-MID-P05 | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_S2025_Midterm.pdf]] | 7 | derived | Pages 7–8 check only the single-node basis and omit the separate two-node HBT constructor. | Q-2025S-MID-P08-a | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_S2025_Midterm.pdf]] | 12 | derived | Page 12 gives only the forward direction, dividing d(a-b)=cdk by d. The reverse implication required by “if and only if” is absent; the note independently supplies multiplication by d. |
| Q-2025S-MID-P08-b | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_S2025_Midterm.pdf]] | 12 | derived | Page 12 asserts the inverse of 14 modulo 23 is 5 but omits all extended Euclidean steps explicitly required by the prompt. The note independently supplies the division chain, back-substitution and full solution family. |
| Q-2025S-FINAL-P05-c | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_S2025_Final.pdf]] | 6 | official | Page 7 replaces the finite exponent sum by its infinite limit and writes $T(n)=n+n\log\log n$. The exact leaf coefficient is n/2, not n; n=4 gives 6 rather than 8. The printed requested O(n log log n) conclusion remains correct, since n is a valid upper bound for n/2. This secondary equality slip is disclosed while retaining official provenance and the complete finite-sum derivation. |
| Q-2025F-MID-P01-a | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2025_Midterm.pdf]] | 3 | official | Page 4 temporarily drops the NAND bars in the redundant equality substituting q→r by ¬q∨r. The defining line, following De Morgan line and final statement retain NAND correctly. The unbarred substitution equality is itself true for ordinary AND, but should use NAND to remain the same expression in this derivation. The correct official conclusion and complete proof are unchanged. |
| Q-2025F-MID-P02-c-premise2 | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2025_Midterm.pdf]] | 7 | official | Page 8 omits grouping parentheses in ∃xE(x)∧¬S(x). Its very next proof line instantiates E(x₀)∧¬S(x₀), demonstrating intended whole-conjunction scope. The explicit form is ∃x(E(x)∧¬S(x)); under narrow immediate-formula parsing the ungrouped second x would be free. This is a secondary notation clarification, not a different official premise. |
| Q-2025F-MID-P02-c-proof | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2025_Midterm.pdf]] | 7 | official | Page 8 omits grouping parentheses in the existential premise and conclusion. The printed proof selects the same fresh x₀ for E and ¬S, applies C(x₀)→S(x₀), derives ¬C(x₀), and generalizes E(x₀)∧¬C(x₀). Thus it establishes the intended ∃x(E(x)∧¬C(x)) conclusion; explicit whole-conjunction parentheses clarify a secondary notation slip rather than replace the official proof. |
| Q-2025F-FINAL-P09-a | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2025_Final.pdf]] | 14 | official | Page 15 prints log₄3≈1.584; the correct approximation is0.79248. | Q-2025F-FINAL-P09-b | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2025_Final.pdf]] | 14 | official | Page 16 has a stray double minus in the last displayed numerator16n²−−3n^c. The announced induction theorem and page15 closed form already have that single minus, and the complete same-proof substitution is correct. This local transcription typo retains official provenance; it is not a different induction conclusion. |

The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $p_n=\frac12+\frac12(3/4)^n$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** Use $p_0=1$ and iterate $p_n=(3/4)p_{n-1}+1/8$: $p_n=(3/4)^np_0+(1/8)\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(3/4)^j=(3/4)^n+\frac12(1-(3/4)^n)$.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 初始概率 $p_0=1$。从 $p_n=(3/4)p_{n-1}+1/8$ 逐次展开，得 $p_n=(3/4)^np_0+(1/8)\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(3/4)^j$。有限等比和为 $4[1-(3/4)^n]$，故 $p_n=(3/4)^n+\tfrac12[1-(3/4)^n]=\tfrac12+\tfrac12(3/4)^n$。代入 $n=0$ 得一，代入 $n=1$ 得 $7/8$，同时满足初值与递推。 |

Review round 1 probability-model check: Pair-up at random does not specify a uniform distribution on the 8! complete boy-girl matchings. The marginal and conditional requested probabilities are underdetermined. The intended uniform permutation model gives P(E)=7!/8!=1/8 and P(F\|E)=6!/7!=1/7. For a full-support alternative, give John partner Mary with probability t in (0,1) and each other girl probability (1-t)/7. Conditional on E, pair Jack with Jill with probability s in (0,1) and each other available girl probability (1-s)/6; complete the other matches uniformly. Conditional on not E, complete the remaining matching uniformly. Every matching is possible, everyone participates exactly once, but P(E)=t and P(F\|E)=s. Taking t=s=1/2 differs from both intended results. Parts (a,d) remain pure counts independent of these weights. |

Intended independent daily trials give E(T)=1/p=120 and P(T<=120)=1-(119/120)^120=0.6336587344..., rounding to 0.6337, not the printed 0.6322. For (a), any fixed ticket can receive winning probability t in (0,1), assigning (1-t)/119 to every other triple, a full-support random draw. For (b,c), even granting uniform winning triples each day, choose one uniform triple U once and repeat it every day while Tom buys the same fixed ticket. Each day still has win probability 1/120; T=1 with probability 1/120 and T=infinity otherwise. Thus E(T)=infinity and P(T<=120)=1/120. The numerical 0.6337 correction is valid only in the intended iid model and is audit-only, not an admitted derived answer.<br>Atom Q-2013-MID-P06-a intended-model work retained for comparison only (not a verified answer to the underdetermined prompt):<br>Provenance: official; supplied source conclusion checked on PDF page(s) 9. The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $1/\binom{10}{3}=1/120\approx0.0083$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** The drawn winning set is uniformly one of $\binom{10}{3}=120$ unordered 3-subsets. Tom's fixed ticket matches exactly one set.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 开奖是从十个号码中均匀抽取三个不同号码，顺序不影响中奖。样本空间共有 $\binom{10}{3}=120$ 个三元素子集，Tom 的票只对应其中一个。因此中奖概率为 $1/120=0.008333\ldots$，按题目要求保留四位小数是 $0.0083$。 |

A geometric waiting time has expectation $1/p=120$.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 按原解答所用的每日独立开奖模型，每日中奖率 $p=1/120$。令 E 为从今天起直到首次中奖的期望天数；首日一定花一天，失败的概率 $1-p$ 下还要平均等待 E 天，故 $E=1+(1-p)E$。移项得 $pE=1$，于是 $E=1/p=120$ 天。这是平均等待时间，不能作为保证中奖的期限。 |

Complementing gives $1-(119/120)^{120}\approx0.6337$. The source page prints $0.6322$ after first replacing $1/120$ by the displayed decimal $0.0083$; that is a rounded-model decimal, whereas $0.6337$ is the independently evaluated exact expression.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 取前题独立开奖模型下 $k=120$。每日不中奖概率为 $119/120$，连续 120 天都失败的概率是 $(119/120)^{120}>0$，故不能保证中奖。取补集得到 $1-(119/120)^{120}\approx0.6337$，保留四位小数仍为 $0.6337$。PDF 第九页先将日成功率舍入为 $0.0083$，再给出 $0.6322$；此处按精确的 $1/120$ 独立重算，明确属于 derived 修正，不将修正数值归为原卷答案。 |

Let $X$ be the number of locations that Tom checks until he finds the gift. For example, $X=1$ if Tom finds the gift after checking just one location and $X=n$ if he finds the gift after checking $n$ locations.<br><br>Replacement context for (b): Now suppose that, each time after Tom checks a location and does not find the gift there, the gift is taken away from its current location and randomly replaced at any of the $n$ locations.<br><br>Note that the gift might be replaced at one of the locations that Tom has checked before. Thus a location might be checked more than once, and the number of checks might be different from the number of locations checked.<br> | Subtopic - Random Variables - 随机变量. The gift is hidden randomly and randomly replaced, without uniform initial or conditional replacement probabilities. The distributions, moments and variance comparison require missing model information. See the recorded intended-model derivation and countermodel; no unique answer is admitted. | Review round 1 model audit: Intended uniform nonrevisiting search has P(X=k)=1/n, E(X)=(n+1)/2 and V(X)=(n^2-1)/12. Intended independent uniform replacement after failure gives geometric(1/n), E(X)=n and V(X)=n(n-1). Initial n=2 hidden-location probabilities t,1-t with a fixed search order instead give E(X)=2-t and V(X)=t(1-t), changing every part-(a) result. For part (b), always inspect location 1, assign probability q in (0,1) to hiding there initially and at each fresh independent replacement, and split the remainder positively among the other locations. Then X is geometric(q), E=1/q and V=(1-q)/q^2, not forced to use q=1/n. For the comparison, even grant uniform initial hiding at n=3: nonrevisiting search has variance 2/3. In (b), inspect location 1 every time and after each failure replace there with probability 9/10 and at each other location with probability 1/20. X=1 with probability 1/3 and 1+G otherwise, G geometric(9/10), so V(X)=(2/3)(10/81)+(2/9)(100/81)=260/729<2/3. This is full-support replacement and reverses the intended comparison. At n=1 the intended variances are equal, not strictly different.<br>Atom Q-2013-MID-P07-a-distribution intended-model work retained for comparison only (not a verified answer to the underdetermined prompt):<br>Provenance: official; supplied source conclusion checked on PDF page(s) 11. The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $P(X=i)=1/n$ for $i=1,\ldots,n$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** The hidden location is uniformly distributed over the $n$ positions. Checking without repetition finds it on check $i$ exactly when it was hidden at position $i$ in the checking order.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 礼物在 n 个位置中等可能地藏于一个位置，不重复检查时，每个隐藏位置都唯一对应检查顺序中的一个发现时刻 i。故 $P(X=i)=1/n$（$i=1,\ldots,n$），其余整数上的概率为零。总概率 $\sum_{i=1}^n1/n=1$，这给出离散均匀分布；若以累积分布表示，则 $P(X\le k)=k/n$，$1\le k\le n$。 |

The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $n(n-1)$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** For a geometric variable on $1,2,\ldots$ with $p=1/n$, $V(X)=(1-p)/p^2=(1-1/n)n^2=n(n-1)$.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 重放后每次条件成功率是 $p=1/n$，从一次起计的几何变量 Y 满足 $P(Y=i)=p(1-p)^{i-1}$。标准几何分布方差为 $(1-p)/p^2$，代入得 $V(Y)=(1-1/n)n^2=n(n-1)$。当 $n=1$ 时立即找到，方差为零，也与公式一致。 |

X=1 with probability 1/3 and 1+G otherwise, G geometric(9/10), so V(X)=(2/3)(10/81)+(2/9)(100/81)=260/729<2/3. This is full-support replacement and reverses the intended comparison. At n=1 the intended variances are equal, not strictly different.<br>Atom Q-2013-MID-P07-c intended-model work retained for comparison only (not a verified answer to the underdetermined prompt):<br>Provenance: official; supplied source conclusion checked on PDF page(s) 12. The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** Part (b): $n(n-1)>(n^2-1)/12$ for $n\ge2$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** The original model removes checked locations, constraining the possible waiting time to $1,\ldots,n$. Replacement leaves every trial with the same chance and an unbounded geometric tail, so its variance is larger; the formulae confirm it.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 原模型不重放，检查会排除位置，次数局限于一至 n；重放模型始终可能失败，等待有更长的尾部。具体比较两方差：$n(n-1)-(n^2-1)/12=(n-1)(11n-1)/12$。对 $n\ge2$ 两因子均正，所以 (b) 方差更大；退化情形 $n=1$ 时两者都为零。 |

Thus $E(X)=(-1)(5/6)+(1+2+3+4+5+6)/36=(-30+21)/36=-1/4$.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 两骰共有 36 个等可能有序结果。不同点数的 30 个结果各亏一元；相同点数 d 的结果每个概率 $1/36$，收益为 d。故 $E(X)=(-1)(30/36)+(1+2+3+4+5+6)/36=(-30+21)/36=-1/4$ 元。概率和 $30/36+6/36=1$。 |

The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $-n/4$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** If $S_n=X_1+\cdots+X_n$, linearity gives $E(S_n)=\sum E(X_i)=n(-1/4)$.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 把 n 局的总收益记为 $S_n=X_1+\cdots+X_n$。每局有相同收益分布，所以每个 $E(X_i)=-1/4$。期望线性性给 $E(S_n)=\sum_{i=1}^nE(X_i)=n(-1/4)=-n/4$。此步相加期望本身无需各局独立。 |

The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $475n/144$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** Independent game outcomes have zero covariance, so $V(S_n)=\sum V(X_i)=n(475/144)$.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 按重复掷骰游戏的独立各局模型，令总收益 $S_n=X_1+\cdots+X_n$。每局方差 $475/144$，不同局的协方差为零。因此 $V(S_n)=\sum_iV(X_i)+2\sum_{i<j}\operatorname{Cov}(X_i,X_j)=n\cdot475/144$。独立性用于消去协方差，不能仅由单局同分布推出方差相加。 |

Inclusion–exclusion counts $P(\bigcup_jG_j)$ and complementing gives the result.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 设 $G_j$ 为第 j 名学生三十天都没有贴纸。若指定 k 名学生均未获贴纸，则每天只能选其余 20−k 人，概率为 $((20-k)/20)^{30}$，这样的 k 人组有 $\binom{20}k$ 个。容斥给 $P(\bigcup_jG_j)=\sum_{k=1}^{20}(-1)^{k+1}\binom{20}k((20-k)/20)^{30}$。所有人都至少得到一次是此并集的补集，所以答案是上述和从一中减去。 |

Literal atomic request: There are 4 aces in a standard deck of 52 cards. David draws a card at random. He keeps the card if it is an ace, but puts the card back to the deck if it is not an ace. The deck is then reshuffled. The process continues until David gets all the 4 aces.<br><br>What is the expected number of draws until David gets all the 4 aces? Explain how your answer is derived.<br>Shared printed wording:  | Subtopic - Random Variables - 随机变量. At-random draws and reshuffling do not specify the uniform next-card law used for the coupon-collector expectation. See the recorded intended-model derivation and countermodel; no unique answer is admitted. | Review round 1 model audit: Intended uniform draws with r aces remaining have success probability r/(48+r) and expected stage length (48+r)/r. Summing r=4,3,2,1 gives 13+17+25+49=104. As a full-support alternative reshuffle, with r>=1 choose the next top card to be an ace with probability 1/2 (uniform among remaining aces), otherwise a non-ace with probability 1/2 (uniform among 48 non-aces); arrange the other cards uniformly. Redraw freshly after returning a non-ace, and remove a drawn ace as required. Every remaining card and permutation is possible. Each of the four stages then has geometric(1/2) mean 2, so total expectation is 8. Randomness alone does not select 104.<br>Atom Q-2014-MID-P08 intended-model work retained for comparison only (not a verified answer to the underdetermined prompt):<br>Provenance: official; supplied source conclusion checked on PDF page(s) 10. The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $13+17+25+49=104$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** When $r$ aces remain, every draw succeeds with probability $r/(48+r)$ because the 48 non-aces are replaced but collected aces are not. The expected waits are $52/4=13$, $51/3=17$, $50/2=25$, and $49/1=49$; add them by linearity.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 把过程分成分别收集第一、二、三、四张 ace 的四阶段。普通牌始终有 48 张，ace 被保留，所以剩 r 张 ace 时牌堆有 48+r 张，每次成功概率 $r/(48+r)$，该阶段期望等待 $(48+r)/r$。依次 r=4,3,2,1 得 $52/4=13$、$51/3=17$、$50/2=25$、$49/1=49$。由期望线性性，总期望为 $13+17+25+49=104$。 |

The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $P(X=k)=(2k-1)/36$ for $k=1,\ldots,6$; otherwise 0.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** There are 36 equally likely ordered outcomes. For integer $1\le k\le6$, $P(X\le k)=k^2/36$. Thus $P(X=k)=[k^2-(k-1)^2]/36=(2k-1)/36$. The probabilities have numerators $1,3,5,7,9,11$, summing to 36. The full CDF is 0 below 1, $\lfloor t\rfloor^2/36$ for $1\le t<6$, and 1 for $t\ge6$; the source uses “distribution function” for the displayed mass table.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 按两颗公平骰子的通常独立掷骰模型，有 36 个等可能有序结果。对整数 $1\le k\le6$，最大值不超过 k 当且仅当两骰均不超过 k，故 $P(X\le k)=k^2/36$。相减得 $P(X=k)=[k^2-(k-1)^2]/36=(2k-1)/36$。六个分子 $1,3,5,7,9,11$ 合为 36，归一化正确。源卷的 distribution function 指概率质量表；若按累计分布定义，则 t<1 时零，$1\le t<6$ 时 $\lfloor t\rfloor^2/36$，t≥6 时一。 |

Literal atomic request: (a) What is the probability to have a sum larger than 5 (excluding 5) when the two dice are tossed at the same time?<br>Shared printed wording: You are given two fair dice where each dice has the number 1 to 6 printed on it. You are also given one wheel so that a random spin of the wheel returns an integer from 2 to 12 in a uniform distribution. Answer the following questions.<br> | Subtopic - Introduction to Probability - 概率基础. The two fair dice do not have a printed within-pair joint law, and the uniformly distributed wheel spins do not have printed cross-spin independence. The requested win probabilities/comparison depend on those missing laws. See the recorded intended-model derivation and countermodel; no unique answer is admitted. | Review round 1 model audit: Intended independent dice in (a) give P(D1+D2>5)=26/36=13/18. Coupling D1=D2=U with U uniform 1,...,6 gives 4/6=2/3 instead. Intended independent wheel spins in (c) give P(X1+X2>=10)=100/121. Coupling both spins to the same uniform U in 2,...,12 gives 8/11 instead, preserving the stated uniform spin marginals. In (d), intended four independent dice give P(sum>=10)=65/72>100/121, so the source prefers dice. Couple all four dice to one fair U instead: probability 2/3<100/121 for independent wheel spins, reversing that preference. Alternatively each dice pair (U,7-U) always totals 7, so two such rolls win certainly. Part (b) remains 35 by linearity from five stated mean-7 spin marginals; no independence is required.<br>Atom Q-2016-FINAL-P01-a intended-model work retained for comparison only (not a verified answer to the underdetermined prompt):<br>Provenance: official; supplied source conclusion checked on PDF page(s) 3. The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $26/36=13/18$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** Sums 2, 3, 4, and 5 occur in $1,2,3,4$ ordered ways, respectively. Thus $P(S>5)=1-(1+2+3+4)/36=26/36$. Directly summing the frequencies for sums 6 through 12 gives $5+6+5+4+3+2+1=26$, checking the complement count.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 通常独立公平掷骰模型共有36个等可能有序对。补事件是和为2、3、4、5，各有1、2、3、4个结果，合10个。因此 $P(S>5)=1-10/36=26/36=13/18$。直接数和6至12又给 $5+6+5+4+3+2+1=26$，与补集计数吻合；和本身不是等可能。 |

The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $100/121$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** Two independent wheel results give $11^2=121$ equally likely ordered pairs. Failure means $X_1+X_2\le9$. For first results $2,3,4,5,6,7$, there are $6,5,4,3,2,1$ possible second results, respectively; larger first results cannot fail. Hence $P(\text{win})=1-21/121=100/121$. This equals the official partition $3/11+67/121$.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 按通常独立重复转盘模型，两转有 $11^2=121$ 个等可能有序对。每步正数，所以第一转已赢时想象继续转并不改变胜负；成功等价于两步总和≥10。失败总和≤9，首转2、3、4、5、6、7时第二转分别有6、5、4、3、2、1种失败值，合21种。因此 $P(\text{win})=1-21/121=100/121$。源解按一转赢3/11和恰两转赢67/121相加，亦为100/121。 |

Part (b) remains 35 by linearity from five stated mean-7 spin marginals; no independence is required.<br>Atom Q-2016-FINAL-P01-d intended-model work retained for comparison only (not a verified answer to the underdetermined prompt):<br>Provenance: official; supplied source conclusion checked on PDF page(s) 4. The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** No. The dice win with probability $65/72$, greater than the wheel's $100/121$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** Imagine both plays even if the first already wins; positive increments make the success event unchanged. For dice $D_1,\ldots,D_4$, failure is $\sum D_i\le9$. Put $Y_i=D_i-1$. Then $\sum Y_i\le5$ and $0\le Y_i\le5$; the upper bounds are automatic. Adding a slack variable gives $\binom{9}{4}=126$ failures among $6^4=1296$ outcomes. Thus $P(\text{win})=1-126/1296=65/72$. The difference is $65/72-100/121=665/8712>0$, verifying the official comparison.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 胜率不同，掷两骰更高。想象完整掷两轮，因增量正，失败正好是四个独立骰子总和≤9。令每骰减一得 $Y_i=D_i-1$，则非负且总和≤5，各自≤5自动满足。加松弛变量后非负解数为 $\binom94=126$，总结果数 $6^4=1296$。胜率 $1-126/1296=65/72$，与轮盘差 $65/72-100/121=665/8712>0$。两种器具均值同为七并不足以决定门槛概率；这里用完整分布比较。 |

The Chinese derivation expands the reasoning.<br><br>**Answer.** $P(A_2\mid A_1)=5/6$.<br><br>**Derivation/proof.** Conditional on each die, rolls are independent, so $P(A_1\cap A_2)=\frac12(\frac13)^2+\frac12(1)^2=5/9$. Divide by $P(A_1)=2/3$ to obtain $(5/9)/(2/3)=5/6$. Alternatively $P(S\mid A_1)=3/4$, giving $\frac34+\frac14\frac13=5/6$.<br><br>### 中文解答<br><br>**推导。** 给定骰子类别后按通常重复掷骰模型独立。普通骰两次均≤2的概率为 $(1/3)^2$，特殊骰为一，混合得 $P(A_1\cap A_2)=(1/2)(1/3)^2+1/2=5/9$。由条件概率定义除以 $P(A_1)=2/3$，得到 $P(A_2\mid A_1)=(5/9)/(2/3)=5/6$。不能把未给类别的两个边缘概率直接相乘。 |

| Q-2016-FINAL-P08 | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2016-final.pdf]] p.15 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened all19 PDF pages, including cover/instructions1–2, all question/solution pages3–16, and scrap pages17–19. Exact p15 prompt: “For this problem, you may assume that n is a non-negative power of 3. Suppose that function T(n) satisfies T(1)=7 and, for n>1, T(n)≤9T(n/3)+5n. Prove that T(n)=O(n²) by advanced induction.” No nonnegativity or runtime assumption is printed. | Induction and asymptotic notation are in syllabus, but the missing lower bound/convention leaves this admission unresolved. | Official pp15–16 proves the valid strengthened upper bound T(n)≤(19/2)n²−(5/2)n: base19/2−5/2=7; substituting the bound at n/3 gives (19/2)n²−(15/2)n+5n. Counterexample to absolute-value Big-O: define T(1)=7 and T(n)=−n³ for powers3 with n≥3. At3, −27≤9·7+15=78. At n≥9, RHS=−n³/3+5n, greater than−n³. Thus all printed inequalities hold, but \|T(n)\|/n²=n is unbounded. No unprinted runtime assumption is supplied. |

| Q-2016-MID-P07-a | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2016-midterm.pdf]] p.11 | not admitted | Task11 reopened rendered PDF page11. Exact shared definition: “If we say that three people in each family must sit together, this means that one member in this family sits at a seat between the two other members in the family.” Printed context gives n families of mother, father and child and3n seats; no uniform random seating or adjacency condition. Item request: What is the probability that k (1≤k≤n) specified families end up sitting together (regardless of whether the other n−k families sit together or not)? | Counting/probability is in syllabus but the missing model and different literal event prevent verified admission. | Official intended uniformly random row and adjacent-family-block formula: (3n−2k)!(3!)^k/(3n)!. At n=2,k=1 this is1/5, whereas literal betweenness in a row is automatic for each triple and gives1. In any linear ordering of three distinct seats there is a unique middle member, regardless of gaps. Thus even choosing a uniform row model cannot make the printed betweenness definition equal adjacency. No unprinted model is admitted. |

| Q-2017-FINAL-P05-b | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2017-final.pdf]] p.8 | not admitted | Task11 reopened PDF pages8–9. Shared game: each player uses their own coin for all three flips; fair coin has head probability1/2, fake coin80%; friend picks either with equal probability, flips first, and gets heads. Literal item(b): Given that your friend’s first coin flip returns heads, what is the probability that you will win the game? (Use your answer in part(a).) No independence statement is printed. | Subtopic - Conditional Probability - 条件概率; Subtopic - Introduction to Probability - 概率基础; Subtopic - Random Variables - 随机变量; Subtopic - Permutations and Combinations - 排列与组合. Admission unresolved because The printed coin game gives marginal head probabilities and random coin allocation but no independence across flips. The conditional winning probability is therefore not uniquely determined. | Official pp8–9 intended independent-flip answer: friend-fake posterior8/13, friend-fair5/13. Friend-fake branch (1/5)²·(1/2)+2·(4/5)(1/5)·(1/8)=3/50; friend-fair branch (1/4)·(112/125)+(1/2)·(64/125)=12/25. Weighted total (8/13)(3/50)+(5/13)(12/25)=72/325=2.88/13. Countermodel: draw independent uniforms U_fair,U_fake once per coin, and on every flip show heads iff U_fair<1/2 or U_fake<4/5 respectively. Allocate coins independently with equal probabilities. Each printed marginal and the single-observation posterior8/13 remain correct. Conditional on the friend’s first head, all three friend flips are heads, so you cannot strictly beat them: win probability0, not72/325. |

| Q-2017-FINAL-P07 | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2017-final.pdf]] p.11 | not admitted | Task11 reopened PDF page11. Ten people at a party decide to randomly choose one person to clean up by rock,paper,scissors. Rules: each simultaneously chooses one symbol R,P,S (R beats S,S beats P,P beats R); if exactly two symbol groups appear, losing-group players are eliminated; otherwise a tie eliminates no one; continue until only one player remains. Literal request: Assuming all players choose symbols randomly, what is the probability that no one is eliminated after ten rounds of the game? The previous note had added “independently” and “uniformly”, neither printed. | Subtopic - Introduction to Probability - 概率基础; Subtopic - Basic Counting - 基本计数; Subtopic - Inclusion-Exclusion - 容斥原理. Admission unresolved because The printed random-symbol description specifies neither uniformity nor independence between players or rounds. Different valid couplings yield different probabilities of ten consecutive ties. | Official p11 intended iid-uniform model: for a fixed pair of symbols, probability exactly both appear=(2/3)^10−2(1/3)^10. Three disjoint pairs give elimination probability3[(2/3)^10−2(1/3)^10]. Independence across rounds gives ten-tie probability [1−3((2/3)^10−2(1/3)^10)]^10≈0.59. Countermodel: in each round draw a common uniform U from{R,P,S} and every player shows U. All players individually choose randomly and uniformly, but choices are coupled. Every round is monochromatic, hence a tie, so no one is eliminated after ten rounds with probability1. The intended iid result is strictly below1. |

| Q-2017-FINAL-P12-a | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2017-final.pdf]] p.16 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened PDF page16. Literal algorithm: positive integer n; 1. Initiate L={2,3,4,…,n}, the list of integers from2 to n. 2. Set p to be the smallest integer of L. Remove p from L and put p in P. Also remove (p+k)p from L for all k≥0 where (p+k)p≤n. 3. Repeat Step2 unless the smallest integer of L is larger than √n. 4. Finally, put all remaining integers in L into P, and return P. No empty-list termination rule is printed. Literal item(a): Prove that P contains the set of all primes up to n. | Subtopic - GCDs and Congruences - 最大公因数与同余; Subtopic - Algorithms - 算法; Subtopic - Inference and Proofs - 推理与证明. Admission unresolved because For allowed positive inputs n=1 and n=2, the printed sieve asks for the minimum of an empty list and is not a total algorithm. Its claimed returned output cannot be verified without adding a missing guard. | Source p16 intended invariant: primes start in L, moving p to P preserves L∪P, and only composite products p(p+k), p≥2,k≥0, are deleted. Intended converse: for composite c≤n with least prime factor p, write c=pu, u≥p; p≤√c≤√n survives until selected, and deletes c=p(p+k). The printed strict n′>p1 excludes prime squares: c=p² has u=p, so ≥ is necessary. Literal execution is undefined at n=1: L=∅ and Step2 asks its minimum. At n=2, Step2 moves2 to P, leaving L=∅; Step3 asks its minimum. At n=3, after moving2, minL=3>√3 causes termination, so this case does return {2,3}; it is not an empty-list failure. n=4 also returns correctly after removing4 and retaining3. The unhandled n=1 and2 cases suffice to invalidate the claimed algorithm on all positive integers. No empty-list guard or restricted input range is silently supplied. |

Then you randomly choose another ball again from the first bag. Scenario2: You randomly choose one of the bags, you randomly choose one ball from that bag and then you put it back. Then you randomly choose another ball from the same bag. E1 is first red and E2 second red. For both scenarios, calculate P(E1),P(E2),and P(E2\|E1). Show the steps of your calculations. The requested Scenario1 P(E1) is not determined by these words alone. | Official p10 intended uniform/conditionally-independent model: scenario1 marginals1/10 and joint1/100, hence conditional1/10; scenario2 equal bags give marginals(1/2)(1/10)+(1/2)(9/10)=1/2, joint(1/2)(1/10)²+(1/2)(9/10)²=41/100, hence conditional41/50. This atom’s intended answer is 1/10. Countermodel: Choose the red ball with probability1/5 and each of the9 blue balls with probability4/45. Every ball can be randomly selected, yet P(E1)=1/5 instead of1/10. |

| Q-2018-FINAL-P09-b | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2018-final.pdf]] p.13 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened p13. Literal prompt: if n=pq where p,q are prime integers, prove exactly(p−1)(q−1) residues in Z_n have multiplicative inverses. No distinctness condition is printed. | GCDs, modular inverses and inclusion-exclusion are in scope, but the missing distinct-prime hypothesis prevents verification of the literal claim; retain needs-review. | Intended distinct-prime proof on p13: units are exactly residues divisible by neither p norq. Among0..pq−1, q multiples ofp andp multiples ofq intersect only at0, so nonunits totalp+q−1 and units pq−p−q+1=(p−1)(q−1). The printed claim that every nonunit gcd is p orq also overlooks gcd(0,pq)=pq, although its sets include0 and the corrected counting proof handles it. Concrete literal counterexample p=q=2: residues1,3 are the two units modulo4, but(p−1)(q−1)=1; generally units modp² numberp²−p, not(p−1)². No distinctness is inserted. |

| Q-2019-FINAL-P03-a | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2019-final.pdf]] p.5 | not admitted | PDF p5, visually reopened: “The algorithm below is the Euclidean algorithm for finding the greatest common divisor of two integers m and n (assuming m≥n).” Literal algorithm gcd(m,n): x←m; y←n; while y≠0: r←x mod y; x←y; y←r; return x. The full source has no positive-input convention. (a) “Let y_i denote the value y at the beginning of the i-th iteration of the while loop. Let k denote the total number of iterations. Show that y_i>2y_(i+2) for i=1,…,k−2.” | Subtopic - GCDs and Congruences - 最大公因数与同余; Subtopic - Algorithms - 算法; Subtopic - Inference and Proofs - 推理与证明. Admission unresolved because Printed input domain is two integers m,n assuming only m≥n, not positive integers. Negative allowed inputs either falsify the required strict remainder inequality under a nonnegative-remainder convention or invoke an undefined mod operation under a positive-divisor-only convention. Cannot silently add positivity. | Intended positive-input proof: x_(i+1)=y_i and y_(i+2)=y_i mod y_(i+1), hence y_i=q y_(i+1)+y_(i+2), q≥1 and 0<y_(i+2)<y_(i+1), giving y_i≥y_(i+1)+y_(i+2)>2y_(i+2). Concrete literal-domain counterexample under nonnegative Euclidean remainders: allowed (m,n)=(3,−5) gives (x,y)=(3,−5)→(−5,3)→(3,1)→(1,0), so k=3 and y_1=−5, y_3=1; claimed −5>2 is false. If mod is defined only for positive divisors, the first operation is undefined instead. Neither convention licenses the proof for all printed integers. |

| Q-2019-FINAL-P12-a | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2019-final.pdf]] p.15 | not admitted | PDF p15, visually reopened: “Suppose the time line is discretized into intervals, with the length of each interval being 1 second. A web server has probability p (0<p<1) of getting 1 visit in each time interval and probability 1−p of getting 0 visit.” No independence is printed. (a) “How long is the server expected to wait until it gets 100 visits?” | Subtopic - Random Variables - 随机变量; Subtopic - Introduction to Probability - 概率基础. Admission unresolved because Per-second Bernoulli marginals do not assert independence across seconds. The official geometric interarrival calculation100/p requires the unprinted independent-trials model, while correlated marginals can give an infinite waiting-time expectation. | Official p15 intended independent model: each interarrival T_i is geometric on positive seconds, E(T_i)=Σ_(k≥0)(1−p)^k=1/p, and linearity gives E(T_1+…+T_100)=100/p. Countermodel: take one B~Bernoulli(p) and set every second I_t=B. Every printed marginal is preserved. If B=1 the 100th visit arrives at second100; if B=0 it never arrives, so T_100=∞ with probability1−p>0 and E(T_100)=∞, not100/p. Part(b) remains3600p under both models. |

| Q-2020-FINAL2-BONUS-a-advantage | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2020-final-part-2.pdf]] p.5 | not admitted | Rendered PDF p5: “During a game show, two players are competing for a prize according to the following rule. They take turns to answer a randomly chosen question, and if the answer is wrong, the player will be eliminated. The player who remains last will be the winner. Suppose each question can be answered correctly with probability1/2 by any player.” No independence or conditional-history rule is stated. “If not, who has an advantage?” | Subtopic - Conditional Probability - 条件概率. Admission unresolved because The source gives only per-question/player correctness probability1/2, without independence across answers. Its official memoryless rotation equations, eventual termination and positional winning probabilities do not follow from those marginals. Terminating coupled models instead give fair2-player or uniform3-player winners; quarantine rather than invent a joint law. | Official intended independent-answer solution on p6: two players terminate a.s. since surviving t questions has probability2^(−t); p1=p2/2 and p1+p2=1 yield p1=1/3,p2=2/3, hence unfair with second-player advantage. For three players, q1=q3/2, q2=q1/2+1/6 and q3=q2/2+1/3, with q1+q2+q3=1, give (5/21,6/21,10/21). The q3 branch after player1 elimination is second among the two remaining players and contributes (1/2)(2/3)=1/3; after player1 succeeds, player3 becomes second among three and contributes q2/2. Deferred Task6 Chinese context correction is retained explicitly: 玩家1被淘汰后，玩家3是剩下两人中的后手，获胜概率为2/3，而不是三人局的位置概率。 Terminating two-player countermodel: draw fair B once; every potential player1 answer is B and every potential player2 answer is1−B. Each question/player has correct-answer marginal1/2. If B=0, player1 immediately fails and player2 wins; if B=1, player1 succeeds and player2 fails. The game ends within two turns and each player wins1/2, making it fair with no positional advantage. Terminating three-player countermodel: choose W uniformly from{1,2,3}; W always answers correctly; conditional on W, each other player has independent Bernoulli(1/4) potential answers. Every player/question has unconditional correctness1/3+(2/3)(1/4)=1/2. Each nonwinner eventually fails almost surely, W never fails, so each player wins1/3. These are legitimate alternative joint laws with all printed marginals, not imported independent models. A third countermodel makes all potential answers one common fair B: B=1 gives nontermination with probability1/2, so normalization of eventual winners is not automatic. |

| Q-2021-FINAL2-P03-a | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2021-final-part-2.pdf]] p.2 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened PDF page2. Shared literal assumption: “Assume that all 7 days of the week are equally likely to be the birthday.” No independence across people is stated. Literal item(a): “What is the probability that two people chosen at random were born on the same day of the week?” | Subtopic - Introduction to Probability - 概率基础. Admission unresolved because Equal weekday marginals do not imply independent birthdays; the probability of a matching pair is not determined by the printed assumptions. | Intended independent-uniform model on page2: (a) 7(1/7)²=1/7; (b) complement of five different weekdays is 1−(7·6·5·4·3)/7⁵=14287/16807≈0.8501; (c) collision probabilities for3 and4 are19/49<1/2 and223/343>1/2, and monotonicity gives minimum4. Concrete shared-U countermodel: choose U uniformly from the seven weekdays and assign every person weekday U. Every individual still has each weekday with probability1/7, but any pair/group of at least2 collides with probability1, so the requested minimum is2. This preserves the printed marginals and contradicts each intended answer; independence is not supplied. |

| Q-2021-FINAL2-P04-a | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2021-final-part-2.pdf]] p.3 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened PDF pp2–3. Shared literal wording: “Each respondent picks a question from the two randomly, and answers the question truthfully, but the interviewer doesn’t know which question is picked.” The known constraints are n surveyed people and m≤n/2 smokers. No uniform question-selection probability is stated. Literal item(a): “Suppose a randomly chosen respondent has answered ‘yes’ to the question. Show that the interviewer’s confidence on the respondent being a smoker (i.e., the probability the respondent being a smoker given that his or her answer is ‘yes’) is at most 2/3.” | Subtopic - Conditional Probability - 条件概率. Admission unresolved because Question selection is called random, but the source does not specify equal probabilities for the two questions. A nonuniform but genuinely random dummy probability t=1/4 changes both the posterior bound and the unbiased-estimation equation. Do not silently supply t=1/2. | Intended page3 solution for uniform t=1/2: with uniform respondent selection s=m/n≤1/2, P(S\|Y)=s/[s+(1−s)/2]=2s/(1+s)≤2/3; E[X]=m+(n−m)/2=(n+m)/2, so f(X)=2X−n is unbiased by linearity. Cross-respondent independence is not needed for that expectation. Nonuniform-t countermodel: choose the dummy question independently with probability t=1/4 (both questions retain positive probability). At s=1/2, even with uniform respondent selection, P(S\|Y)=s/[s+(1−s)t]=4/5>2/3. In general E[2X−n]=2m+2(n−m)t−n, so for n=2,m=1,t=1/4 it is1/2 rather than1. No single estimator of X works for all unspecified t: for n=2,m=0 and independent dummy choices, unbiasedness would require f(0)(1−t)²+2f(1)t(1−t)+f(2)t²=0 for every t∈(0,1). Polynomial identity forces f(0)=f(1)=f(2)=0, which cannot have expectation1 when m=1. A known t<1 would instead permit (X−nt)/(1−t); the source does not provide it. |

Cross-respondent independence is not needed for that expectation. At s=1/2, even with uniform respondent selection, P(S\|Y)=s/[s+(1−s)t]=4/5>2/3. In general E[2X−n]=2m+2(n−m)t−n, so for n=2,m=1,t=1/4 it is1/2 rather than1. No single estimator of X works for all unspecified t: for n=2,m=0 and independent dummy choices, unbiasedness would require f(0)(1−t)²+2f(1)t(1−t)+f(2)t²=0 for every t∈(0,1). Polynomial identity forces f(0)=f(1)=f(2)=0, which cannot have expectation1 when m=1. A known t<1 would instead permit (X−nt)/(1−t); the source does not provide it. |

| Q-2022-FINAL1-P01-a | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2022-final-part-1.pdf]] p.1 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened PDF page1. Shared literal wording: “Toss a fair coin 2n times. You earn $1 for each heads, and lose $1 for each tails. Calculate the probabilities of the following events”. Fairness is stated, but independence of tosses is not. Literal item(a): “Your net return is 0.” | Subtopic - Introduction to Probability - 概率基础; Subtopic - Binomial Coefficients - 二项式系数. Admission unresolved because Fairness supplies only single-toss marginals, not independent equiprobable sequences. A common fair outcome changes the probability of zero final return. | Intended iid-fair solution on p1 for positive integer n: with H heads net return2H−2n, so (a) C(2n,n)/2^(2n); (b) C(2n,n−1)/2^(2n); (c) H≡n(mod2), and flipping the first bit pairs opposite parities, giving1/2. At n=0 the empty return is0 and the multiple-of4 event has probability1. For(d), map a bad balanced path to a path ending−2 by keeping its prefix through the first partial sum−1 and reversing every later step. The suffix sum changes+1 to−1, giving endpoint−2. Every path ending−2 first reaches−1, and the same suffix reversal recovers its unique bad balanced preimage. Thus the count is C(2n,n)−C(2n,n−1)=C(2n,n)/(n+1), and iid probability divides by2^(2n). Concrete shared-coin countermodel: choose one fair sign B∈{−1,+1} and set every toss equal toB. Every individual toss is fair. At n=1, (a) has probability0 instead of1/2, (b) probability1/2 instead of1/4, and(d) probability0 instead of1/4. At n=2 all net returns are±4, so(c) has probability1 instead of1/2. Counts/reflection remain correct combinatorics, but equal sequence weights do not follow from fairness alone. |

| Q-2022-FINAL1-P05-a | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2022-final-part-1.pdf]] p.3 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened PDF pp3–4. Shared literal wording: “Five couples (i.e., 10 people) sit down at random in a row of 10 seats.” No uniform probability on the10! arrangements is stated. Literal item(a): “Two of the 5 couples are Peter/Mary and John/Helen. Since Peter and John are friends, they want to sit next to each other. What is the probability that Peter and Mary sit together, John and Helen sit together, and Peter and John sit together?” | Subtopic - Introduction to Probability - 概率基础; Subtopic - Permutations and Combinations - 排列与组合. Admission unresolved because Random seating is not explicitly uniform over arrangements; a uniform random cyclic rotation preserves individual uniform seat marginals but changes the three required adjacencies to probability7/10. | Intended uniform10!-seating model on pp3–4: (a) required links M−P,P−J,J−H form a consecutive four-person block with only MPJH and HJPM orders; seven units give2·7!/10!=1/360. (b) five couple blocks have5! orders and2^5 orientations, giving5!2^5/10!=1/945. The source first sentence in(b) literally says “6 couples”, a secondary typo inconsistent with both the five-couple prompt and its own five-block computation; retain the defect, do not change the cohort. (c) for specified k adjacent couples compress into10−k units with2^k orientations, so inclusion-exclusion gives [sum(k=0..5)(−1)^k C(5,k)2^k(10−k)!]/10!, with unspecified couples free. Concrete rotation countermodel: uniformly choose one of the10 cyclic rotations of Mary,Peter,John,Helen,A,a,B,b,C,c, where A/a,B/b,C/c are the remaining couples. Each individual occupies each seat with probability1/10. The three required adjacency edges for(a) survive7 of10 possible row cuts, giving7/10; all five couple edges survive5 cuts, giving1/2 for(b); every cut leaves at least4couples adjacent, giving0 for(c). To give positive probability to every seating, mix this rotation distribution with the uniform10! distribution in equal weights; all marginals remain uniform, but each requested probability is the average of the two differing values. Thus “random” without arrangement weights does not determine the printed probabilities. |

| Q-2022-FINAL2-P01 | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/2022-final-part-2.pdf]] p.1 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened all four PDF pages, especially p1 literal prompt: Prove that in an undirected graph G=(V,E), if \|E\|>C(\|V\|−1,2), then G is connected. No simple-graph restriction occurs here or in general instructions, unlike explicit simple in Q5/Q6. | Graph connectivity, extremal edge counting and proof are in scope, but the required graph convention is missing; retain the approved needs-review disposition. | Intended simple-graph proof on p1: a disconnected component of k vertices leaves n−k others, so \|E\|≤C(k,2)+C(n−k,2)≤C(n−1,2) for1≤k≤n−1. The final inequality follows because the difference C(n−1,2)−C(k,2)−C(n−k,2)=(k−1)(n−k−1)≥0, with equality at the endpoint sizes. Thus the strict edge threshold forces connectivity for simple graphs. The source explicitly warns against assuming the extremal split without proof, and this factorization supplies it. Concrete countermodel for the printed broader undirected graph: vertices a,b,c, two parallel ab edges, isolated c. It has2>C(2,2)=1 edges but is disconnected. No missing simplicity assumption is inserted. |

| Q-2023F-FINAL-P02-a-i | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2023_Final.pdf]] p.4 | not admitted | Task11 viewed PDF p4: There are 5 blue balls and 3 green balls in a basket. Every time, we close our eyes and pick a ball at random from the basket (and we will not put the ball back in the basket after picking it). The words at random do not specify equal conditional probabilities for remaining balls. The intended answer and rubric are on p4–5. Literal target: What is the probability that we see a green ball for the first time in our 4th pick? Show the steps to calculate the final answer as a single fraction like a/b. | Subtopic - Introduction to Probability - 概率基础; Subtopic - Conditional Probability - 条件概率. Admission unresolved because Picking at random without replacement does not explicitly supply a uniform conditional selection law. The official first-green-fourth result3/28 requires one; weighted full-support blind sampling gives1/22, so the requested probability is not determined by the printed conditions. | Under intended uniform conditional selection, the event is BBBG and successive conditional probabilities are5/8,4/7,3/6,3/5, whose product is3/28. These are without-replacement conditional probabilities, not independent draws. Full-support countermodel: at each draw, each remaining blue ball has weight1 and each green weight2, selected proportional to weight without replacement. This can be blind selection with differently weighted physical balls, and every labeled permutation has positive probability. First green4 then has probability(5/11)(4/10)(3/9)(6/8)=1/22, not3/28. Summing the eight possible first-three color histories gives probability100/231 for a green fourth ball, not3/8. Uniform conditional selection is an additional model, not a consequence of closing the eyes. |

| Q-2023F-FINAL-P03 | in-scope | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2023_Final.pdf]] p.6 | official | Task11 direct source review: Viewed p6 prevalence1/10000, sensitivity99.9%, false positive0.02%, negative-test target and complete Bayes expression. | Q-2023F-FINAL-P04-c | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2023_Final.pdf]] p.7 | not admitted | Task11 viewed p7 literal: Assume K is a random variable. Why is it not possible that K>E(K) for all possible values of K? In other words, explain why there must be values for K such that K≤E(K). Neither this item nor the complete source restricts K to the finite bulb experiment or states a finite expectation. | Subtopic - Random Variables - 随机变量; Subtopic - Inference and Proofs - 推理与证明. Admission unresolved because The printed K is an arbitrary random variable, with no finite real expectation assumption. The claimed lower-than-mean witness fails when EK=−∞ and is meaningless when EK is undefined. The finite-mean proof is valid but cannot supply an omitted hypothesis. | Intended finite-mean proof: set μ=EK∈R; if Z=K−μ>0 everywhere then events A_j={Z≥1/j} have union the whole space. At least one has positive probability, implying EZ≥P(A_j)/j>0, contradicting EZ=EK−μ=0. Countermodel P(K=−2^j)=2^(−j), j≥1, is normalized since the geometric sum is1; EK=−Σ1=−∞, so all finite K>EK and no possible K≤EK. Also a symmetric ±2^j law with probability2^(−j−1) at each sign has both positive and negative expectation parts infinite, making EK undefined. The finite switch existence proof in(d) remains valid locally without this unqualified claim. |
| Q-2023F-FINAL-P04-d | in-scope | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2023_Final.pdf]] p.7 | official | Task11 direct source review: Viewed p7 existence statement and hint permitting(c), official n/2 conclusion; no source assumption of independent flips is imposed. Review round 1 visually reopened page 7 and inspected the stated probability/model conditions. | Construct an independent fair switch experiment, pair opposite bulb states, sum expectations, and apply the lower-witness result to minus Y. | Task11 compared the official conclusion to cited source/solution page(s) 7. Independent check: This existence proof may choose its own uniform distribution on2^m configurations. For each bulb toggle one fixed incident switch to pair ON/OFF configurations, giving EX_i=.5; linearity EY=n/2. If every finite configuration had Y<n/2 their finite average would be strictly smaller, contradiction. Thus some Y≥ceil(n/2). Local finite-average proof removes dependence on quarantined arbitrary-variable(c); no independence between bulbs required. Review round 1 probability-model check: Retained (d) is deterministic existence. A proof may deliberately average over all 2^m switch subsets: toggling one incident switch pairs ON and OFF states of each bulb, so some subset lights at least n/2 bulbs. This constructed finite averaging law is not an assumption about the unspecified random mechanism in quarantined (a,b). |

| Q-2023F-FINAL-P11 | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2023_Final.pdf]] p.17 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened p17 and checked full20-page source. Literal: Give a contrapositive proof that if there are exactly two odd degree vertices in a simple graph, then there is a path between them. In other words, those two nodes have to be in the same connected component. The parenthesis defines simple as undirected, unweighted, without multiple edges or loops, possibly connected or disconnected, and refers to illustrative simple/nonsimple graphs. It does not say finite. The old note source commentary called this the general finite simple graph in the question; that was an added assumption, not printed wording. The original example figure remains in the Paper audit context. | Subtopic - Graphs - 图论基础; Subtopic - Inference and Proofs - 推理与证明. Admission unresolved because The printed simple-graph definition does not restrict the graph to be finite. Two disjoint one-way infinite rays have exactly two odd-degree endpoints but no path between them. Finite-component handshaking parity proves the intended statement only under an unprinted finiteness condition; admission remains unresolved. | Intended finite proof by contraposition: if odd-degree u,v have no path, they belong to disjoint components C_u,C_v. In each finite component, sum(degrees)=2\|E\| gives an even number of odd vertices; hence C_u has another odd u′ and C_v another odd v′. These four distinct vertices contradict exactly two odd vertices. Countermodel without finiteness: V={a_i,b_i:i≥0}, E={{a_i,a_(i+1)},{b_i,b_(i+1)}:i≥0}. Vertices a_0,b_0 have degree1, all other vertices degree2, there are no loops or multiple edges, and the two components have no connecting path. The infinite degree sum has no even-integer parity conclusion. This does not alter fixed-n finite graph questions. |

| Q-2023F-MID-P07 | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2023_Midterm.pdf]] p.16 | not admitted | Task11 viewed p16 exact wording: Imagine the communication infrastructure of HKUST contains n antennas in a line. Due to a typhoon, m of them are broken at random and are not functioning. The whole communication system remains functional if no two consecutive antennas are broken. What is the probability that the communication system remains functional? No uniform subset law is stated; p16 answer/rubric use a counting ratio. | Subtopic - Introduction to Probability - 概率基础; Subtopic - Basic Counting - 基本计数; Subtopic - Permutations and Combinations - 排列与组合. Admission unresolved because The source says m antennas are broken at random, without specifying a uniform distribution over the m-subsets. The official favorable/total count assumes such a distribution. Even identical one-antenna breakage marginals and full support do not determine the requested functioning probability. | The intended uniform-subset model has C(n,m) equally likely broken sets. There are n−m functioning antennas and n−m+1 gaps; choose m distinct gaps for the broken antennas, giving the valid combinatorial count C(n−m+1,m), hence intended ratio C(n−m+1,m)/C(n,m), with0 when gaps insufficient and1 for m0. Countermodel n=4,m=2: choose {1,3} or {2,4} equally, yielding functioning probability1; instead choose {1,2} or {3,4} equally, yielding0. In both models each antenna is broken with marginal1/2, while uniform all6 subsets yields3/6=1/2. Mix either countermodel with weight ε∈(0,1) of the uniform law: every subset now has positive probability, all marginals stay1/2, but functioning probabilities are1−ε/2 and ε/2. Thus even full support/equal marginals cannot supply the omitted subset distribution. |

Always picking {1,2,3,4} gives P(range=3)=1; uniform selection gives 97/C(100,4). The printed answer page 9 also says k−2 interior choices although its formula C(k−1,2) is correct. The absent sampling model cannot be repaired silently; needs-review. | Task11 nonadmission recheck. Always choosing {1,2,3,4} instead gives P(range3)=1, versus 97/C(100,4). Missing sampling weights remain unresolved. |

Uniform card with red revealed whenever available conditions equally on RR and RB, giving 1/2. No unique answer without observation protocol. |

The official 1/64 assumes all 2^9 colorings equiprobable. A single fair color chosen once and assigned to all nine tiles has fair color marginals and rotational invariance probability 1. This genuine alternative joint model makes the requested probability underdetermined; needs-review. The example carpet is readable on page 9. | Task11 nonadmission recheck. One common fair color for all nine tiles preserves fair marginals and gives invariance probability1. Quarantine remains. |

| Q-2024F-FINAL-P04-a | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2024_Final.pdf]] p.12 | not admitted | Task11 direct source review: PDF p12 arbitrary random variable X and Var(aX)=a²Var(X); no finite-moment condition. p13 uses expectation algebra. | Subtopic - Random Variables - 随机变量. Probability / Random Variables supports variance scaling only when the relevant moments exist. The prompt assumes an arbitrary random variable X and supplies no existence or finite-variance condition. For the discrete law P(X=2^j)=P(X=-2^j)=2^(-j-1), j>=1, total probability is one but the positive and negative parts of E(X) both diverge, so E(X) and Var(X) are undefined. Even a=1 leaves an undefined expression, not a universal numerical identity. Page 13 silently applies expectation algebra; restricting X to finite variance would add an assumption. Consistently with Spring Final 3(b), this is needs-review and has no Question note. | Task11 nonadmission recheck. Independent check: The printed algebra is valid with finite variance. The discrete law P(X=±2^j)=2^(−j−1), j≥1, has total mass1 but both signed mean parts diverge, so mean and variance are undefined even for a=1. No general identity is admitted by silently imposing moments. |
| Q-2024F-FINAL-P04-b-i | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2024_Final.pdf]] p.13 | not admitted | Task11 direct source review: PDF p13 k phone numbers, each location uniformly random among n; no independent placements; asks empty locations. | Subtopic - Random Variables - 随机变量. Probability / Random Variables: the prompt asks the expected number of locations with no phone numbers stored but only specifies each phone number has a uniformly random location. The source answer assumes independent locations and prints n(1−1/n)^k. Let k=n=2 and store both in the same single uniformly chosen location: both marginals are uniform, yet the no phone numbers stored count is n−1, whereas independence gives 1/2. Marginal uniformity cannot justify the product probabilities, and no Question is published without the missing independence condition. | Task11 nonadmission recheck. Independent check: Independent intended indicators give n(1−1/n)^k. For k=n=2, put both numbers at one common uniform location: each marginal is uniform, but empty count1 differs from iid expectation1/2. Remains underdetermined. |

Common uniform location at k=n=2 gives zero singleton boxes instead of iid expectation1. Quarantine unchanged. |

| Q-2024F-FINAL-P10 | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_F2024_Final.pdf]] p.26 | not admitted | Task11 visually reopened PDF p26 and checked the full31-page source. Literal: Assume n is of form n=2^k for an integer k. Consider the following recurrence relation: T(n)=3T(n/2)+n, n>1; T(1)=1. Iterate the recurrence to get the closed form solution for T(n). No nonnegative-k qualification is printed. The old note retained that wording but limited its derived answer to k≥0; that does not determine the requested values on the full literal domain. | Subtopic - Recursion - 递归. Admission unresolved because The prompt permits n=2^k for any integer k, but only defines T(1)=1 and T(n)=3T(n/2)+n for n>1. At k=−1, T(1/2) is unconstrained: values0 or1 both satisfy all printed conditions. The intended closed form requires k≥0. The unprinted domain restriction cannot be supplied silently; needs-review. | For k≥0 only, valid iteration is T(n)=3^iT(n/2^i)+n sum(j=0..i−1)(3/2)^j; at i=k this equals3^k+2n[(3/2)^k−1]=3n^(log₂3)−2n. Checks T1=1 and T2=5. The printed denominator2^(k−i) in sum(i=0..k−1)3^i n/2^(k−i) is wrong: k1 yields n/2 rather than n. The source later uses the correct geometric sum but never assembles final T(n). For the literal all-integer-k domain, define the valid formula on k≥0 and assign arbitrary values on k<0; T(1/2)=0 or1 leaves every printed recurrence/base equation unchanged. Therefore no unique full-domain closed form follows. Intended solution retained as audit evidence, not an admitted answer. |

A full response thus requires continuous expectation/integration outside this baseline. The printed claim also lacks finite-expectation assumptions: independent symmetric variables with P(X=2^j)=P(X=-2^j)=2^(-j-1), j≥1, have undefined E(X). No Question is published. | Task11 nonadmission recheck. Independent check: Existing out-of-scope disposition retained: full demanded scope includes continuous expectation beyond baseline. The intended integrable independent-product argument factors the product-law expectation into EX·EY. | Q-2024S-FINAL-P03-b | needs-review | [[knowledge_base/past_paper/COMP2711_S2024_Final.pdf]] p.3 | not admitted | Task11 direct source review: p3 printed covariance definition uses EX,EY but gives no moment existence assumption; p4 rubric checked. | Subtopic - Random Variables - 随机变量. Probability / Random Variables supplies expectation linearity and independent products, and the printed covariance definition is usable algebraically only when its expectations exist. No such hypothesis is printed. Take independent X,Y with P(X=2^j)=P(X=-2^j)=2^(-j-1), j≥1: positive and negative expectation parts both diverge, so E(X), E(Y), and Cov(X,Y) are undefined rather than zero. The official proof silently assumes finite moments. Needs clarification; no finite/discrete restriction is inserted. | Task11 nonadmission recheck. Independent check: Existing needs-review retained: independent copies with P(X=±2^j)=2^(-j−1),j≥1 have undefined means, hence covariance undefined rather than0. Intended finite/integrable proof expands covariance to E(XY)−EX·EY then uses independence; this conditional argument is audit evidence only, not a proof of the unrestricted printed claim. |
